import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Seed handling'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

ID derivation JAH-EQPYS-000026 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

ID derivation computes each generated record's ID from its seed: ID = GEN_PREFIX + str(100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000), so IDs are stable, unique per seed, and checkable without a lookup. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

ID derivation computes each generated record's ID from its seed: ID = GEN_PREFIX + str(100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000), so IDs are stable, unique per seed, and checkable without a lookup. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator ID derivation — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Apply the formula inside generate(), stamp the resulting ID on the record, and verify in validate() that the ID starts with the generator's own prefix. Two different seeds must never collide on one ID. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Seed sensitivity is proven by the template self-test (seeds 7 and 8 give different IDs); every pilot demo prints its seed-derived ID.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'ID derivation'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Deterministic execution JAH-EQPYS-000027 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Deterministic execution means generate(seed) run twice produces byte-identical output — the property that makes generated records verifiable at all. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Deterministic execution means generate(seed) run twice produces byte-identical output — the property that makes generated records verifiable at all. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator deterministic execution — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Run generate(seed) twice in one process and assert equality; run it again in a fresh process and compare hashes. Any nondeterminism (timestamps, dict ordering, random calls) is removed from the output path, and the demo asserts Deterministic: MATCH. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Determinism is proven for every pilot demo (both SHA-256 digests match) and for the template (8/8 self-tests).

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Deterministic execution'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Record validation JAH-EQPYS-000028 · simple

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Validation checks a generated record before it is accepted: all required keys present, ID prefix correct, score in range, seed an integer — with every failure named. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Validation checks a generated record before it is accepted: all required keys present, ID prefix correct, score in range, seed an integer — with every failure named. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator record validation — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Run validate(record) over every generated record; refuse any record with a non-empty problem list and report the problems by name. Validation never silently repairs a bad record. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

The template validator accepts the good record and rejects a 4-key stub with named problems; every pilot demo asserts zero validation problems.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Record validation'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Content-hash dedupe JAH-EQPYS-000029 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Content-hash dedupe stops the archive filling with copies: every generated record is hashed, and a record whose hash already exists in the archive is refused as a duplicate. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Content-hash dedupe stops the archive filling with copies: every generated record is hashed, and a record whose hash already exists in the archive is refused as a duplicate. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator content-hash dedupe — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Hash the record's canonical JSON (sorted keys), look the hash up in the archive index, and refuse duplicates with the existing record's ID cited. The hash covers content only, never timestamps. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

The template self-test proves the hash is stable across runs and changes when the content changes; the CLI refuses duplicate hashes.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Content-hash dedupe'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Input schema JAH-EQPYS-000030 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

The input schema states exactly what the generator accepts — seed (integer), topic (title from the topic table), count (1-100) — so a caller can build a valid call without reading the source. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

The input schema states exactly what the generator accepts — seed (integer), topic (title from the topic table), count (1-100) — so a caller can build a valid call without reading the source. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator input schema — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Declare the inputs in the CLI help and in the companion-record schema; validate every call against them before running. A call that fails validation is refused with the violation named. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

The template CLI enforces the throttle (count above 100 is refused with exit code 2) and the schema file validates the record shape.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Input schema'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Output schema JAH-EQPYS-000031 · simple

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

The output schema states exactly what the generator returns — id, title, seed, totals, coverage, pass rate, score — so any consumer can parse the output without guessing. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

The output schema states exactly what the generator returns — id, title, seed, totals, coverage, pass rate, score — so any consumer can parse the output without guessing. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator output schema — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Emit one JSON object per record with the fixed keys, sorted for stability; the schema file pins the keys and their types. A record missing a key fails validation. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

The output schema is pinned in companion-record-schema.json and proven by every pilot demo's printed record.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

Run 1 SHA-256: fb0faf137215c21ded76a8722d771b31e4d9a0b192f58c683a0e7c9c7a1b8e4a
Run 2 SHA-256: fb0faf137215c21ded76a8722d771b31e4d9a0b192f58c683a0e7c9c7a1b8e4a

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Output schema'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Error codes JAH-EQPYS-000032 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Error codes give every generator failure a stable exit status — 2 for a bad call, 3 for a validation failure, 4 for a duplicate — so a caller can handle failures instead of guessing. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Error codes give every generator failure a stable exit status — 2 for a bad call, 3 for a validation failure, 4 for a duplicate — so a caller can handle failures instead of guessing. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator error codes — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Return the code with a human sentence on stderr; never return a bare traceback to a caller. Codes are drawn from the template's list, never invented per pilot. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Error codes are implemented in the template CLI (exit 2/3/4) and inherited by every pilot generator.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Error codes'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Batching and throttle JAH-EQPYS-000033 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Batching caps one call at 100 records (throttle_per_call) so a runaway call cannot flood the archive; larger jobs are split into batches of 100. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Batching caps one call at 100 records (throttle_per_call) so a runaway call cannot flood the archive; larger jobs are split into batches of 100. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator batching and throttle — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Enforce the throttle in the runner: any count above 100 is refused before generation starts. The limit is recorded in every record's generator field. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

The throttle is proven by the template CLI (count 101 is refused) and recorded in all 288 pilot records.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

Generated: {"checked": 8, "coverage_pct": 0.0, "covered": 100, "id": "JAH-EQPY-GEN-155433", "pass_rate_pct": 100.0, "passed": 8, "score": 50.0, "seed": 7, "title": "Batching and throttle", "total": 1000000000}

Run 2 SHA-256: 50342f625efa441815e627ad49e496202d7278d01a72f2c2dc5e5f2928e91879
Deterministic: MATCH

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 1000000000, 'covered': 100, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Batching and throttle'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Versioning JAH-EQPYS-000034 · simple

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Versioning stamps the pattern version (v1.0.0, 2026-11-12) into the generator, so a record can always be traced to the exact pattern version that made it. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Versioning stamps the pattern version (v1.0.0, 2026-11-12) into the generator, so a record can always be traced to the exact pattern version that made it. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator versioning — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Record the pattern version in PATTERN.md and in each pilot's manifest entry; when the pattern changes, the version changes and old generators stay runnable so old records stay regenerable. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Pattern v1.0.0 is the version stamped on all three pilots; no earlier version exists.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Versioning'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Generator chaining JAH-EQPYS-000035 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Chaining feeds one generator's output into the next generator's input — a coverage generator feeding a validation generator — so a pipeline can run as one job. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Chaining feeds one generator's output into the next generator's input — a coverage generator feeding a validation generator — so a pipeline can run as one job. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator generator chaining — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Declare each link's output schema and the next generator's input schema, and check they match before running. A link whose schemas mismatch is refused before any generation happens. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Chaining is a design in this record: the pilots' generators are standalone, and no chained run is claimed.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Generated: {"checked": 8, "coverage_pct": 0.0, "covered": 0, "id": "JAH-EQPY-GEN-155433", "pass_rate_pct": 100.0, "passed": 8, "score": 50.0, "seed": 7, "title": "Generator chaining", "total": 63048}

Run 2 SHA-256: 750e1be8b710e33166240cc14f3aeea60d5313064204dbf78ada5edc4f41183a

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 0, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Generator chaining'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Output archiving JAH-EQPYS-000036 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Output archiving stores accepted records with their seed, generator version, and content hash, so any archived record can be verified or regenerated later. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Output archiving stores accepted records with their seed, generator version, and content hash, so any archived record can be verified or regenerated later. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator output archiving — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Write each accepted record to the companion database's index with its seed and hash; an archived record is never edited in place — a correction is a new record that cites the old one. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Archiving is the companion database itself: 288 pilot records stored as gz indexes with their seeds and hashes.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 288, 'covered': 288, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Output archiving'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Command-line interface JAH-EQPYS-000037 · simple

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

The CLI is how a human runs the generator: --seed, --topic, --count, --self-test, with exit codes that say what happened. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

The CLI is how a human runs the generator: --seed, --topic, --count, --self-test, with exit codes that say what happened. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator command-line interface — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Parse arguments with argparse, run the requested generation or self-test, and print one JSON record per line to stdout. Errors go to stderr with an exit code, never to stdout mixed with records. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

The template CLI is proven by execution (--self-test 8/8, --seed 7 --count 2 prints two valid records).

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Command-line interface'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Configuration JAH-EQPYS-000038 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Configuration is the generator's fixed settings — prefix, hard stop, throttle, topic table — kept in the program itself, so a run needs no external config file to be reproducible. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Configuration is the generator's fixed settings — prefix, hard stop, throttle, topic table — kept in the program itself, so a run needs no external config file to be reproducible. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator configuration — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Keep GEN_PREFIX, HARD_STOP, THROTTLE_PER_CALL, and TOPICS as module constants; a run with the same constants and seed reproduces the same output. Settings are never read from the environment. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Configuration-as-constants is the template's design and is proven by the self-test's reproducibility.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Configuration'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Logging JAH-EQPYS-000039 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Logging records what the generator did — seeds run, records made, failures — to stderr, so a run can be audited without polluting the records on stdout. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Logging records what the generator did — seeds run, records made, failures — to stderr, so a run can be audited without polluting the records on stdout. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator logging — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Print records to stdout (one JSON object per line) and all diagnostics to stderr; a run's log states its seed range and its record count. Logs are never mixed into record output. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Logging discipline is implemented in the template CLI; pilot demos print only the generated record and the digests.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Logging'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Self-tests JAH-EQPYS-000040 · simple

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Self-tests prove the generator works before it is trusted: determinism, validation, seed sensitivity, score range, hash stability, and throttle — eight checks, all passing. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Self-tests prove the generator works before it is trusted: determinism, validation, seed sensitivity, score range, hash stability, and throttle — eight checks, all passing. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator self-tests — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Run run_tests() before accepting any generator; a single failing check blocks the generator. The test list is fixed in the template and inherited by every pilot. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Self-tests are proven: template 8/8 PASS, and every pilot demo re-runs the equivalent checks (determinism + validation) on its own records.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Self-tests'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Self-tests'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):

        second = self.generate(seed)

        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Benchmarking JAH-EQPYS-000041 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Benchmarking measures how fast the generator runs — records per second on the reference machine — so slow generators can be found and slow calls avoided. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Benchmarking measures how fast the generator runs — records per second on the reference machine — so slow generators can be found and slow calls avoided. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator benchmarking — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Time a standard batch (100 records) and report the elapsed time with the machine's description. A benchmark without its machine description cannot be compared with anything. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Benchmarking is a design in this record: pilot generators run in milliseconds per record, and no formal benchmark was measured — the record does not invent one.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo
