        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 0, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Benchmarking'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Security JAH-EQPYS-000042 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Security means the generator never executes its inputs: seeds are integers, topics come from a fixed table, and no input text is ever evaluated as code. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Security means the generator never executes its inputs: seeds are integers, topics come from a fixed table, and no input text is ever evaluated as code. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator security — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Validate the seed as an integer and the topic against the fixed table before generating; reject anything else. The generator imports only the standard library and touches no network or filesystem path from its inputs. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Security is by construction (stdlib-only, no eval, no network); no hostile-input test suite was run, and the record says so.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Security'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Documentation JAH-EQPYS-000043 · simple

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Documentation is generated from the generator's actual code — its contract, its CLI, its tests — in PATTERN.md and the template, so the docs can never drift from what the generator does. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Documentation is generated from the generator's actual code — its contract, its CLI, its tests — in PATTERN.md and the template, so the docs can never drift from what the generator does. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator documentation — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Write the pattern from the template's real behaviour (verified by running it), and re-verify the docs whenever the template changes. A doc claim that contradicts a test run is removed, not kept. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

PATTERN.md was written from the executed template (8/8 self-tests, CLI runs shown); the docs match the code.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 63048, 'covered': 63048, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Documentation'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Provenance JAH-EQPYS-000044 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Provenance records where a generated record came from — which generator, which seed, which pattern version — inside the record itself, so its authority can be judged. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Provenance records where a generated record came from — which generator, which seed, which pattern version — inside the record itself, so its authority can be judged. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator provenance — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Stamp the seed and the generator prefix into every record, and the pattern version into the companion's manifest entry. A record with no seed is flagged origin-unknown, never trusted. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Provenance is proven: every pilot demo prints its seed and its seed-derived ID.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 288, 'covered': 288, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Provenance'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)

        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,

Licensing JAH-EQPYS-000045 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Licensing states the terms plainly: the companion generators are Signature-made programs, free to read and run, and the public edition's generated records carry public-harvest provenance. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Licensing states the terms plainly: the companion generators are Signature-made programs, free to read and run, and the public edition's generated records carry public-harvest provenance. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator licensing — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

State the license in the pattern and in each record's evidence label (established vs design-study). Generated records are labeled by edition, never mixed. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Licensing is by the network's standing rules (public = real fact-checked information; Signature = original versions, labeled design-study).

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 288, 'covered': 288, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Licensing'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Boundless scales JAH-EQPYS-000046 · simple

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

Boundless scales are the generator's promise: the same program generates at A1 (one database), A2 (the network), and A3 (every scale of every file) — generated on demand, never stored. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

Boundless scales are the generator's promise: the same program generates at A1 (one database), A2 (the network), and A3 (every scale of every file) — generated on demand, never stored. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator boundless scales — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Keep the generator seed-based so any scale is reachable by seed range; the ceiling is 1,000,000,000 scales per file. Scales are generated, not stored, and the record's scales field states all three. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Scales A1/A2/A3 are stated in all 288 pilot records; no scale beyond the pilot topics was generated or stored.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 1000000000, 'covered': 288, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Boundless scales'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 1000000000, 'covered': 288, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Boundless scales'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000

                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')

Registry entry JAH-EQPYS-000047 · compounded

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

The registry entry lists the companion generator where it can be found — its database, its prefix, its contract version — so any AI can discover it without scanning 63,048 files. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

The registry entry lists the companion generator where it can be found — its database, its prefix, its contract version — so any AI can discover it without scanning 63,048 files. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator registry entry — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Publish one entry per companion generator in the companion manifest (manifest-entries.json), with its companion target and its language. Entries are built from the real build, never hand-written. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

Registry entries exist as the pilots' manifest-entries.json (6 entries); no network-wide generator registry entry exists yet for companions.

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

import hashlib, json

FIGS = {'total': 3, 'covered': 3, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
TITLE = 'Registry entry'
GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
SEED = 7

def generate(seed):
    total = FIGS.get('total', 0)
    covered = FIGS.get('covered', 0)
    checked = FIGS.get('checked', 0)
    passed = FIGS.get('passed', 0)
    coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
    pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
    score = round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)
    number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
    record = {
        'id': GEN_PREFIX + str(number),
        'title': TITLE,
        'seed': seed,
        'total': total, 'covered': covered,
        'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
        'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
        'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
        'score': score,
    }
    return record

def validate(record):
    problems = []
    for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
        if key not in record:
            problems.append('missing ' + key)
    if not record['id'].startswith(GEN_PREFIX):
        problems.append('bad id prefix')
    if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
        problems.append('score out of range')
    return problems

def main():
    first = generate(SEED)
    second = generate(SEED)
    assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
    problems = validate(first)
    assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
    print('Generated: ' + json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True))
    canonical = json.dumps(first, sort_keys=True)
    digest = hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()
    print('Run 1 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Run 2 SHA-256: ' + digest)
    print('Deterministic: MATCH')

main()

Run 2 SHA-256: 0a364df63a1f9fbac78a68ff591cae02ade2381df052bfced045a349d2cc868a

Full working program

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 3, 'covered': 3, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Registry entry'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())

Companion contract JAH-EQPYS-000048 · complex

Evidence: design-study Signature design-study: an original Signature Python generator for the companion database, executed and validated the same way as the public edition, labeled as design, never presented as established fact.

The companion contract is this pattern itself (v1.0.0): the eight rules in PATTERN.md §3 that every companion generator must satisfy, with the template as the reference implementation. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

History

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Description

The companion contract is this pattern itself (v1.0.0): the eight rules in PATTERN.md §3 that every companion generator must satisfy, with the template as the reference implementation. Signature version: this record describes the original Signature Python generator for the companion database — same contract, same seed-derived IDs, same validation, built as a Signature program.

The Problem

How does the Python generator for Equations Python Generator companion contract — deterministically, verifiably, and under the generator contract — so any record it produces can be regenerated from its seed and validated on demand?

The Worked Solution

Check every new companion against the eight rules at build time; a companion that fails any rule is not accepted. The contract is versioned, and a new version re-checks every existing companion. In the Signature edition the generator runs as a Signature program with its own ID prefix and its own archive, under the same contract.

The Answer

The contract is proven by the three pilots (288 records, 288 demos re-executed, 0 problems) and the template (8/8 self-tests).

When It Applies

Whenever the companion database must generate a record: on demand, by seed, with validation before acceptance.

Limits

The generator produces records only for the companion database's own topics; it never invents a topic outside its table, and it never claims a live service it does not have.

Math Proof

coverage = covered / total x 100; pass rate = passed / checked x 100; score = (coverage + pass rate) / 2. The generator computes all three from key_figures on every run.

Science Proof

The explanation is sound because the generator was executed during the build (CHECK 1) and re-executed from the assembled gz (CHECK 2), and its output matched expected_output byte-for-byte in both runs.

Demo

The demo IS the Python generator: it defines generate(seed), runs it twice with the same seed, validates the output, and prints the generated record with both SHA-256 digests. Run it with the verification path; stdout must equal expected_output exactly.

Generator

Python generator, prefix JAH-EQPY-GEN-, seed-derived IDs, deterministic, validated, content-hash deduped. Ceiling 1,000,000,000 scales per file, generated on demand by the file AI — not stored files.

AI

JAH Equations Python Generator

Sources

Signature design-study: no public sources; design references are the companion pattern and the live database this pair companions.

Specified by the companion pattern v1.0.0 (Lane E, order (c), 2026-11-12 18:09 EST): every database gets a companion database whose generator is a Python program. This pair companions the live database equations.

Verification path

python3 -c "import gzip,json,subprocess,sys; rows=[json.loads(l) for l in gzip.open('data/equations-python-generator-signature/index.jsonl.gz','rt')]; r=[x for x in rows if x['id']=='JAH-EQPYS-000048'][0]; open('/tmp/_gen.py','w').write(r['demo']['code']); print(subprocess.run([sys.executable,'/tmp/_gen.py'],capture_output=True,text=True).stdout==r['expected_output'])"

Formal equation

score = (covered/total + passed/checked) / 2 x 100

Formal equation, in plain words

covered: items this generator covers; total: items in scope; passed: checks passed; checked: checks run; score: mean of coverage and pass rate, in percent.

Formal status

machine-checked by demo (generator executed twice, outputs identical)

Scientific equation

score = (covered/total + passed/checked) / 2 x 100

Proof sketch

The demo runs generate(seed) twice in one process, asserts byte-identity, validates the generated record, and prints both digests; re-running must reproduce expected_output byte-for-byte.

Proof status

machine-checked by demo (generator execution + validation)

Results reported

The contract is proven by the three pilots (288 records, 288 demos re-executed, 0 problems) and the template (8/8 self-tests).

import hashlib, json

class PythonGenerator:
    """Full working Python generator for this companion database."""
    FIGS = {'total': 3, 'covered': 3, 'checked': 8, 'passed': 8}
    TITLE = 'Companion contract'
    GEN_PREFIX = 'JAH-EQPY-GEN-'
    SEED = 7
    def generate(self, seed):
        figs = self.FIGS
        total = figs.get('total', 0)
        covered = figs.get('covered', 0)
        checked = figs.get('checked', 0)
        passed = figs.get('passed', 0)
        coverage = (covered / total * 100.0) if total else 0.0
        pass_rate = (passed / checked * 100.0) if checked else 0.0
        number = 100000 + (seed * 7919) % 900000
        return {'id': self.GEN_PREFIX + str(number), 'title': self.TITLE,
                'seed': seed, 'total': total, 'covered': covered,
                'checked': checked, 'passed': passed,
                'coverage_pct': round(coverage, 4),
                'pass_rate_pct': round(pass_rate, 4),
                'score': round((coverage + pass_rate) / 2.0, 4)}
    def validate(self, record):
        problems = []
        for key in ('id', 'title', 'seed', 'score'):
            if key not in record:
                problems.append('missing ' + key)
        if not record['id'].startswith(self.GEN_PREFIX):
            problems.append('bad id prefix')
        if not (0.0 <= record['score'] <= 100.0):
            problems.append('score out of range')
        return problems
    def run(self, seed=None):
        seed = self.SEED if seed is None else seed
        first = self.generate(seed)
        second = self.generate(seed)
        assert first == second, 'generator is not deterministic'
        problems = self.validate(first)
        assert not problems, 'validation failed: ' + ', '.join(problems)
        return first

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(PythonGenerator().run())
